7.2
Light-cone QCD sum rules for the
Y(4500)
as an example

The light-cone QCD sum rules have been applied extensively to study the two-body strong decays of the tetraquark (molecular) states
[
127
,
462
,
467
,
807
,
808
,
809
,
810
,
811
]
, where only the ground state contributions are isolated, and an unknown parameter is (or not) introduced by hand to parameterize the higher resonance contributions, then the Ioffe-Smilga-type trick,

\displaystyle 1-T^{2}\frac{d}{dT^{2}}\,,

(449)

is (or not) adopted to subtract this parameter
[
812
,
813
]
. The Ioffe-Smilga-type trick was suggested to deal with the traditional hadrons, where there exists a triangle Feynman diagram. In the case of tetraquark (molecular) states, we deal with two disconnected loop diagrams approximately, see Fig.
31
, we would like not to resort to the Ioffe-Smilga trick, and write down the higher resonance contributions explicitly.

We would like to use an example to illustrate how to study the strong decays of the tetraquark states via the light-cone QCD sum rules, and write down the three-point correlation function
\Pi_{\mu\alpha\beta}(p)
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mu\alpha\beta}(p,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i^{2}\int d^{4}xd^{4}y\,e^{-ip\cdot x}e^{-iq\cdot y}\,\langle 0|T\left\{J_{\mu}^{Y}(0)J_{\alpha}^{D^{*+}}(x)J^{\bar{D}^{*0}}_{\beta}(y)\right\}|\pi(r)\rangle\,,

(450)

where

\displaystyle J_{\mu}^{Y}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}}{2}\Big[u^{T}_{j}(0)C\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c_{k}(0)\bar{u}_{m}(0)\gamma_{5}\gamma^{\nu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}(0)+u^{T}_{j}(0)C\gamma^{\nu}\gamma_{5}c_{k}(0)\bar{u}_{m}(0)\gamma_{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}(0)

\displaystyle+d^{T}_{j}(x)C\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c_{k}(0)\bar{d}_{m}(0)\gamma_{5}\gamma^{\nu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}(0)+d^{T}_{j}(0)C\gamma^{\nu}\gamma_{5}c_{k}(0)\bar{d}_{m}(0)\gamma_{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}(0)\Big]\,,

\displaystyle J_{\alpha}^{D^{*+}}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{d}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}c(x)\,,

\displaystyle J_{\beta}^{\bar{D}^{*0}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{c}(y)\gamma_{\beta}u(y)\,,

(451)

interpolate the
Y(4500)
,
\bar{D}^{*}
and
D^{*}
, respectively
[
547
]
, the
|\pi(r)\rangle
is the external
\pi
state.

At the hadron side, we insert a complete set of intermediate hadronic states with the same quantum numbers as the interpolating currents into the three-point correlation function, and isolate the ground state contributions,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mu\alpha\beta}(p,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M_{D^{*}}^{2}\frac{-iG_{\pi}r_{\tau}+iG_{Y}p^{\prime}_{\tau}}{(M_{Y}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}\varepsilon^{\rho\sigma\lambda\tau}\left(-g_{\mu\rho}+\frac{p^{\prime}_{\mu}p^{\prime}_{\rho}}{p^{\prime 2}}\right)

(452)

\displaystyle\left(-g_{\alpha\sigma}+\frac{p_{\alpha}p_{\sigma}}{p^{2}}\right)\left(-g_{\lambda\beta}+\frac{q_{\lambda}q_{\beta}}{q^{2}}\right)+\cdots\,,

where
p^{\prime}=p+q+r
, we adopt the standard definitions for the decay constants
\lambda_{Y}
,
f_{D^{*}}
,
f_{\bar{D}^{*}}
, and define the hadronic coupling constants
G_{\pi}
and
G_{Y}
,

\displaystyle\langle Y_{c}(p^{\prime})|\bar{D}^{*}(p)D^{*}(q)\pi(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{\pi}\varepsilon^{\rho\sigma\lambda\tau}\varepsilon^{*}_{\rho}\xi_{\sigma}\zeta_{\lambda}r_{\tau}-G_{Y}\varepsilon^{\rho\sigma\lambda\tau}\varepsilon^{*}_{\rho}\xi_{\sigma}\zeta_{\lambda}p^{\prime}_{\tau}\,\,,

(453)

the
\varepsilon_{\mu}
,
\xi_{\alpha}
and
\zeta_{\beta}
are polarization vectors of the
Y(4500)
,
\bar{D}^{*}
and
D^{*}
, respectively. In the isospin limit,
m_{u}=m_{d}
,
f_{D^{*}}=f_{\bar{D}^{*}}
and
M_{D^{*}}=M_{\bar{D}^{*}}
.

We multiply Eq.(
452
) with the tensor
\varepsilon_{\theta\omega}{}^{\alpha\beta}
and obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{\mu\theta\omega}(p,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\theta\omega}{}^{\alpha\beta}\,\Pi_{\mu\alpha\beta}(p,q)

(454)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M_{D^{*}}^{2}\frac{iG_{\pi}\left(g_{\mu\omega}r_{\theta}-g_{\mu\theta}r_{\omega}\right)-iG_{Y}\left(g_{\mu\omega}p^{\prime}_{\theta}-g_{\mu\theta}p^{\prime}_{\omega}\right)}{(M_{Y}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}+\cdots\,.

Again, we take the isospin limit, then
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\mu\theta\omega}(p,q)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{\mu\theta\omega}(q,p)
, and we write down the relevant components explicitly,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{\mu\theta\omega}(p,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[i\Pi_{\pi}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})-i\Pi_{Y}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})\right]\left(g_{\mu\omega}r_{\theta}-g_{\mu\theta}r_{\omega}\right)

(455)

\displaystyle+i\Pi_{Y}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})\left(g_{\mu\omega}q_{\theta}-g_{\mu\theta}q_{\omega}\right)+\cdots\,,

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{\pi}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M_{D^{*}}^{2}G_{\pi}}{(M_{Y}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle\Pi_{Y}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M_{D^{*}}^{2}G_{Y}}{(M_{Y}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}+\cdots\,.

(456)

Then we choose the tensor structures
g_{\mu\omega}r_{\theta}-g_{\mu\theta}r_{\omega}
and
g_{\mu\omega}q_{\theta}-g_{\mu\theta}q_{\omega}
to study the hadronic coupling constants
G_{\pi}
and
G_{Y}
, respectively. We obtain the hadronic spectral densities
\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s,u)
through triple dispersion relation,

\displaystyle\Pi_{H}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{\prime 2}}^{\infty}ds^{\prime}\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{\infty}ds\int_{\Delta_{u}^{2}}^{\infty}du\frac{\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s,u)}{(s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2})(s-p^{2})(u-q^{2})}\,,

(457)

where the
\Delta_{s}^{\prime 2}
,
\Delta_{s}^{2}
and

\Delta_{u}^{2}
are the thresholds, and we add the subscript
H
to represent the hadron side.

Figure 33:
The lowest order Feynman diagrams, where the dashed (solid) lines denote the heavy (light) quark lines, the ovals denote the external
\pi^{+}
meson.

We carry out the operator product expansion up to the vacuum condensates of dimension 5 and neglect the tiny gluon condensate contributions
[
520
,
789
]
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\pi}(p^{2},q^{\prime 2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\pi}m_{c}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{\pi}(u)\left[\int_{0}^{1}dxx\bar{x}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon-1)}{2\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon-1}}-\frac{2m_{c}\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{3(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})}\right.

(458)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{m_{c}^{3}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{3(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{3}}\right]\frac{1}{(q+ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\bar{u}\left[\int_{0}^{1}dxx\bar{x}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon-1)}{2\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon-1}}-\frac{2m_{c}\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{3(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{m_{c}^{3}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{3(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{3}}\right]\frac{1}{(q+ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{f_{\pi}m_{c}^{2}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{36}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{\pi}(u)\frac{1}{(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})((q+ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}m_{c}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{36(m_{u}+m_{d})}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\bar{u}\frac{1}{(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})((q+ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\Pi_{Y}(p^{2},q^{\prime 2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\left[\int_{0}^{1}dxx\bar{x}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon-1)}{2\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon-1}}-\frac{2m_{c}\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{3(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})}\right.

(459)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{m_{c}^{3}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{3(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{3}}\right]\frac{1}{(q+ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}m_{c}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{36(m_{u}+m_{d})}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\frac{1}{(p^{2}-m_{c}^{2})((q+ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle+f_{3\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}dx\bar{x}\left[\frac{3\Gamma(\epsilon)}{8\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon}}-\frac{p^{2}}{2\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})}\right]\frac{1}{q^{2}-m_{c}^{2}}

\displaystyle-f_{3\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}dxx\bar{x}\left[\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon-1)}{2\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon-1}}+\frac{p^{2}\Gamma(\epsilon)}{2\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon}}\right]\frac{1}{(q^{2}-m_{c}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle-f_{3\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}dxx\left[\frac{3\Gamma(\epsilon)}{8\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})^{\epsilon}}+\frac{p^{2}}{4\pi^{2}(p^{2}-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})}\right]\frac{1}{q^{2}-m_{c}^{2}}\,,

where
q^{\prime}=q+r
,
\bar{u}=1-u
,
\bar{x}=1-x
,
\tilde{m}_{c}^{2}=\frac{m_{c}^{2}}{x}
,
(q-ur)^{2}-m_{c}^{2}=(1-u)q^{2}+u(q+r)^{2}-u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}-m_{c}^{2}
.
And we have used the
\pi
light-cone distribution functions
[
814
]
,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\bar{d}(0)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}u(x)|\pi(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{\pi}r_{\mu}\int_{0}^{1}due^{-iur\cdot x}\varphi_{\pi}(u)+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\bar{d}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}u(x)|\pi(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{6}\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}\left(r_{\mu}x_{\nu}-r_{\nu}x_{\mu}\right)\int_{0}^{1}due^{-iur\cdot x}\varphi_{\sigma}(u)\,,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\bar{d}(0)i\gamma_{5}u(x)|\pi(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}\int_{0}^{1}due^{-iur\cdot x}\varphi_{5}(u)\,,

(460)

and the approximation,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\bar{d}(x_{1})\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}g_{s}G_{\alpha\beta}(x_{2})u(x_{3})|\pi(r)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{3\pi}\left(r_{\mu}r_{\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}+r_{\nu}r_{\beta}g_{\mu\alpha}-r_{\nu}r_{\alpha}g_{\mu\beta}-r_{\mu}r_{\beta}g_{\nu\alpha}\right)\,,

for the twist-3 quark-gluon light-cone distribution functions with the value
f_{3\pi}=0.0035\,\rm{GeV}^{2}
at the energy scale
\mu=1\,\rm{GeV}

[
814
,
815
]
.
Such terms proportional to
m_{\pi}^{2}
and their contributions are greatly suppressed, and we also neglect the twist-4 light-cone distribution functions due to their small contributions. According to the Gell-Mann-Oakes-Renner relation
\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}=-\frac{2\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{f_{\pi}}
, we take account of the Chiral enhanced contributions fully in Eqs.(
458
)-(
459
).

In Fig.
33
, we draw the lowest order Feynman diagrams as an example to illustrate the operator product expansion.

In the soft limit
r_{\mu}\to 0
,
(q+r)^{2}=q^{2}
, we can set
\Pi_{\pi/Y}(p^{2},q^{\prime 2},q^{2})=\Pi_{\pi/Y}(p^{2},q^{2})
, then we obtain the QCD spectral densities
\rho_{QCD}(s,u)
through double dispersion relation,

\displaystyle\Pi^{QCD}_{\pi/Y}(p^{2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{\infty}ds\int_{\Delta_{u}^{2}}^{\infty}du\frac{\rho_{QCD}(s,u)}{(s-p^{2})(u-q^{2})}\,,

(462)

we add the superscript (subscript)
QCD
to stand for the QCD side.

We match the hadron side with the QCD side below the continuum thresholds
s_{0}
and
u_{0}
to acquire rigorous quark-hadron duality
[
520
,
789
]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{\Delta_{u}^{2}}^{u_{0}}du\frac{\rho_{QCD}(s,u)}{(s-p^{2})(u-q^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{\Delta_{u}^{2}}^{u_{0}}du\left[\int_{\Delta_{s}^{\prime 2}}^{\infty}ds^{\prime}\frac{\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s,u)}{(s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2})(s-p^{2})(u-q^{2})}\right]\,,

and we carry out the integral over
ds^{\prime}
firstly,
then

\displaystyle\Pi_{H}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2},q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M_{D^{*}}^{2}G_{\pi/Y}}{(M_{Y}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}+\int_{s^{\prime}_{0}}^{\infty}ds^{\prime}\frac{\tilde{\rho}_{H}(s^{\prime},M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2},M_{D^{*}}^{2})}{(s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}

(464)

\displaystyle+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M_{D^{*}}^{2}G_{\pi/Y}}{(M_{Y}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}+\frac{C_{\pi/Y}}{(M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}-p^{2})(M_{D^{*}}^{2}-q^{2})}+\cdots\,,

where
\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s,u)=\tilde{\rho}_{H}(s^{\prime},s,u)\delta(s-M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2})\delta(u-M_{D^{*}}^{2})
,
and we introduce the parameters
C_{\pi/Y}
to parameterize the contributions concerning the higher resonances and continuum states in the
s^{\prime}
channel,

\displaystyle C_{\pi/Y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s^{\prime}_{0}}^{\infty}ds^{\prime}\frac{\tilde{\rho}_{H}(s^{\prime},M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2},M_{D^{*}}^{2})}{s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2}}\,.

(465)

As the strong interactions among the ground states
\pi
,
D^{*}
,
\bar{D}^{*}
and excited
Y^{\prime}
states are complex, and we have no knowledge about the corresponding four-hadron contact vertex. In practical calculations, we can take the unknown functions
C_{\pi/Y}
as free parameters and adjust the values to acquire flat platforms for the hadronic coupling constants
G_{\pi/Y}
with variations of the Borel parameters. Such a method works well in the case of three-hadron contact vertexes
[
520
,
789
]
, and we expect it also works here.

In Eq.(
452
) and Eq.(
454
), there exist three poles in the limit

p^{\prime 2}\to M_{Y}^{2}
,
p^{2}\to M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}
and
q^{2}\to M_{D^{*}}^{2}
. According to the relation
M_{Y}\approx M_{\bar{D}^{*}}+M_{D^{*}}
, we set
p^{\prime 2}=4q^{2}
and perform double Borel transformation with respect to the variables
P^{2}=-p^{2}
and
Q^{2}=-q^{2}
respectively, then we set
T_{1}^{2}=T_{2}^{2}=T^{2}
to obtain two QCD sum rules,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{YD^{*}D^{*}}G_{\pi}}{4\left(\widetilde{M}_{Y}^{2}-M_{D^{*}}^{2}\right)}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{M_{D^{*}}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)-\exp\left(-\frac{\widetilde{M}_{Y}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)\right]\exp\left(-\frac{M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)+C_{\pi}\exp\left(-\frac{M_{D^{*}}^{2}+M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\pi}m_{c}\int_{m_{c}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{\pi}(u)\left[\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{x_{i}}^{1}dxx\bar{x}(s-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})-\left(\frac{2m_{c}\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{3}-\frac{m_{c}^{3}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{6T^{4}}\right)\delta(s-m_{c}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{s+m_{c}^{2}+u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}\int_{m_{c}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\bar{u}\left[\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{x_{i}}^{1}dxx\bar{x}(s-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})-\left(\frac{2m_{c}\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{3}-\frac{m_{c}^{3}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{6T^{4}}\right)\delta(s-m_{c}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{s+m_{c}^{2}+u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)+\frac{f_{\pi}m_{c}^{2}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{36T^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{\pi}(u)\exp\left(-\frac{2m_{c}^{2}+u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}m_{c}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{36(m_{u}+m_{d})T^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\bar{u}\exp\left(-\frac{2m_{c}^{2}+u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)\,,

(466)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{YD^{*}D^{*}}G_{Y}}{4\left(\widetilde{M}_{Y}^{2}-M_{D^{*}}^{2}\right)}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{M_{D^{*}}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)-\exp\left(-\frac{\widetilde{M}_{Y}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)\right]\exp\left(-\frac{M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)+C_{Y}\exp\left(-\frac{M_{D^{*}}^{2}+M_{\bar{D}^{*}}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{m_{u}+m_{d}}\int_{m_{c}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\left[\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{x_{i}}^{1}dxx\bar{x}(s-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})-\left(\frac{2m_{c}\langle\bar{q}q\rangle}{3}-\frac{m_{c}^{3}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{6T^{4}}\right)\delta(s-m_{c}^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{s+m_{c}^{2}+u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)-\frac{f_{\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}m_{c}\langle\bar{q}g_{s}\sigma Gq\rangle}{36(m_{u}+m_{d})T^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}du\varphi_{5}(u)\exp\left(-\frac{2m_{c}^{2}+u\bar{u}m_{\pi}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{f_{3\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{m_{c}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{x_{i}}^{1}dx\bar{x}\left[\frac{3}{4}+s\,\delta(s-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})\right]\exp\left(-\frac{s+m_{c}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{f_{3\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{2\pi^{2}T^{2}}\int_{m_{c}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{x_{i}}^{1}dxx\bar{x}\,\tilde{m}_{c}^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{s+m_{c}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{f_{3\pi}m_{\pi}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{m_{c}^{2}}^{s_{0}}ds\int_{x_{i}}^{1}dxx\left[\frac{3}{2}-s\,\delta(s-\tilde{m}_{c}^{2})\right]\exp\left(-\frac{s+m_{c}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)\,,

(467)

where
\lambda_{YD^{*}D^{*}}=\lambda_{Y}f_{D^{*}}^{2}M^{2}_{D^{*}}
,
\widetilde{M}_{Y}^{2}=\frac{M_{Y}^{2}}{4}
and
x_{i}=\frac{m_{c}^{2}}{s}
.

In calculations, we fit the free parameters as
C_{\pi}=0.00101(T^{2}-3.6\,\rm{GeV}^{2})\,\rm{GeV}^{4}
and

C_{Y}=0.00089(T^{2}-3.2\,\rm{GeV}^{2})\,\rm{GeV}^{4}

to acquire uniform flat Borel platforms
T^{2}_{max}-T^{2}_{min}=1\,\rm{GeV}^{2}
.
The Borel windows are
T^{2}_{\pi}=(4.6-5.6)\,\rm{GeV}^{2}
and
T^{2}_{Y}=(4.4-5.4)\,\rm{GeV}^{2}
, where the subscripts
\pi
and
Y
represent the corresponding channels. In Fig.
34
, we plot the hadronic coupling constants
G_{\pi}
and
G_{Y}
with variations of the Borel parameters. In the Borel windows, there appear very flat platforms indeed.

Figure 34:
The hadronic coupling constants with variations of the Borel parameters
T^{2}
, where the (I) and (II) denote the
G_{\pi}
and
G_{Y}
, respectively, the regions between the two vertical lines are the Borel windows.

We obtain the hadronic coupling constants routinely,

\displaystyle G_{\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 15.9\pm 0.5\,\rm{GeV}^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle G_{Y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 10.4\pm 0.6\,\rm{GeV}^{-1}\,,

(468)

by setting

\displaystyle\delta\,\bar{\lambda}_{Y}\bar{f}_{D^{*}}\bar{f}_{\bar{D}^{*}}\bar{G}_{\pi/Y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}_{Y}\bar{f}_{D^{*}}\bar{f}_{\bar{D}^{*}}\bar{G}_{\pi/Y}\frac{4\delta G_{\pi/Y}}{\bar{G}_{\pi/Y}}\,.

(469)

It is direct to obtain the partial decay width,

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(Y(4500)\to D^{*}\bar{D}^{*}\pi^{+}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{24\pi M_{Y}}\int dk^{2}(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{4}(p^{\prime}-k-p)\frac{d^{3}\vec{k}}{(2\pi)^{3}2k_{0}}\frac{d^{3}\vec{p}}{(2\pi)^{3}2p_{0}}

(470)

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{4}(k-q-r)\frac{d^{3}\vec{q}}{(2\pi)^{3}2q_{0}}\frac{d^{3}\vec{r}}{(2\pi)^{3}2r_{0}}\Sigma|T|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6.43^{+0.80}_{-0.76}\,\rm{MeV}\,,

where
T=\langle Y_{c}(p^{\prime})|\bar{D}^{*}(p)D^{*}(q)\pi(r)\rangle
.

The partial decay width
\Gamma\left(Y(4500)\to D^{*}\bar{D}^{*}\pi^{+}\right)=6.43^{+0.80}_{-0.76}\,\rm{MeV}
is much smaller than the total width
\Gamma=246.3\pm 36.7\pm 9.4\,\rm{MeV}
from the BESIII collaboration
[
156
]
.

The three-body strong decays of the
Y(4230)
are also studied in this scheme
[
816
]
,
this scheme could be applied to the two-body strong decays of the tetraquark (molecular) states straightforwardly.

We write down the two-point correlation functions

\Pi(p,r)
,

\displaystyle\Pi(p,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\int d^{4}x\,e^{-ip\cdot x}\,\langle 0|T\left\{J_{A}(0)J_{B}(x)\right\}|P(r)\rangle\,,

(471)

where the currents
J_{A}(0)
and
J_{B}(x)
interpolate the tetraquark (molecular) states and traditional mesons, respectively, the
P(r)
are external states.

At the hadron side, we obtain

\displaystyle\Pi(p,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{A}\lambda_{B}G_{ABP}}{(M_{A}^{2}-(p+r)^{2})(M_{B}^{2}-p^{2})}+\cdots\,,

(472)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{H}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2})\,,

where
p^{\prime}=p+r
, and we rewrite the correlation functions
\Pi_{H}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2})
as

\displaystyle\Pi_{H}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta^{2}}^{s_{A}^{0}}ds^{\prime}\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s^{0}_{B}}ds\frac{\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s)}{(s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2})(s-p^{2})}+\int_{s^{0}_{A}}^{\infty}ds^{\prime}\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s^{0}_{B}}ds\frac{\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s)}{(s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2})(s-p^{2})}+\cdots\,,

through double-dispersion relation, where the
\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s)
are the hadronic spectral densities,

\displaystyle\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\lim_{\epsilon_{2}\to 0}}\,\,{\lim_{\epsilon_{1}\to 0}}\frac{{\rm Im}_{s^{\prime}}\,{\rm Im}_{s}\,\Pi_{H}(s^{\prime}+i\epsilon_{2},s+i\epsilon_{1})}{\pi^{2}}\,,

(474)

where the
\Delta^{2}
and
\Delta_{s}^{2}
are the thresholds, the
s_{A}^{0}
and
s_{B}^{0}
are the continuum thresholds.

Then we carry out the operator product expansion (not necessary on the light-cone) at the QCD side, and write the correlation functions
\Pi_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2})
as

\displaystyle\Pi_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s^{0}_{B}}ds\frac{\rho_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},s)}{s-p^{2}}+\cdots\,,

(475)

through single-dispersion relation, where the
\rho_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},s)
are the QCD spectral densities,

\displaystyle\rho_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\lim_{\epsilon_{1}\to 0}}\,\,\frac{{\rm Im}_{s}\,\Pi_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},s+i\epsilon_{1})}{\pi}\,.

(476)

As the QCD spectral densities
\rho_{QCD}(s^{\prime},s)
do not exist,

\displaystyle\rho_{QCD}(s^{\prime},s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\lim_{\epsilon_{2}\to 0}}\,\,{\lim_{\epsilon_{1}\to 0}}\,\,\frac{{\rm Im}_{s^{\prime}}\,{\rm Im}_{s}\,\Pi_{QCD}(s^{\prime}+i\epsilon_{2},s+i\epsilon_{1})}{\pi^{2}}

(477)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

because

\displaystyle{\lim_{\epsilon_{2}\to 0}}\,\,\frac{{\rm Im}_{s^{\prime}}\,\Pi_{QCD}(s^{\prime}+i\epsilon_{2},p^{2})}{\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

(478)

And we will write the QCD spectral densities
\rho_{QCD}(p^{\prime 2},s)
as
\rho_{QCD}(s)
for simplicity.

We match the hadron side with the QCD side of the correlation functions,
and accomplish the integral over
ds^{\prime}
firstly to obtain the rigorous quark-hadron duality
[
520
]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s_{B}^{0}}ds\frac{\rho_{QCD}(s)}{s-p^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s_{B}^{0}}ds\frac{1}{s-p^{2}}\left[\int_{\Delta^{2}}^{\infty}ds^{\prime}\frac{\rho_{H}(s^{\prime},s)}{s^{\prime}-p^{\prime 2}}\right]\,.

(479)

And we write down the quark-hadron duality explicitly,

\displaystyle\int_{\Delta_{s}^{2}}^{s^{0}_{B}}ds\frac{\rho_{QCD}(s)}{s-p^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{A}\lambda_{B}G_{ABP}}{(m_{A}^{2}-p^{\prime 2})(m_{B}^{2}-p^{2})}+\frac{C_{A^{\prime}B}}{m_{B}^{2}-p^{2}}\,.

(480)

Again, we introduce the parameters
C_{A^{\prime}B}
to parameterize the net effects. In numerical calculations, we take the
C_{A^{\prime}B}
as free parameters, and choose suitable values to obtain the stable QCD sum rules with the variations of
the Borel parameters
T^{2}
.

8
Conclusion and Perspective

At the present time, we can only say confidently that the tetraquark and pentaquark states are established in sense of that there are four and five valence quarks, respectively. The under-structures are still under hot debates, more experimental and theoretical works are still needed before reaching definite conclusion. The QCD sum rules method is a reliable and powerful theoretical tool in studying the multiquark states and has given many successful descriptions, however, the predictions have arbitrariness depending on the treating schemes, only comprehensive and systematic works would work.

Appendix

Some typical and useful examples of the Borel transformations,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[(P^{2})^{k}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}\Big[\frac{\Gamma(k)}{(P^{2})^{k}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(T^{2})^{k-1}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}\Big[\frac{\Gamma(k)}{(s+P^{2})^{k}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(T^{2})^{k-1}}\exp\left(-\frac{s}{T^{2}}\right)\,.

(481)

Some useful examples of the Fierz transformations.

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}J_{1^{+-}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{u^{T}_{j}C\gamma_{5}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}-u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma_{5}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(482)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}u-i\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}c\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}u+\bar{c}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c-\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}c-i\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u

\displaystyle+i\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c\,\bar{d}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}u-i\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma_{\nu}c+i\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}u\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,,

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}J_{1^{+-}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\nu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}-u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\nu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(483)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\bar{d}u\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c+i\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u\,\bar{c}c+i\bar{d}c\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u+i\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c\,\bar{c}u

\displaystyle-\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}u-\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}u-\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}u-\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}u

\displaystyle+i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\gamma^{\alpha}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\beta}u-i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\gamma^{\alpha}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\beta}\gamma_{5}u

\displaystyle+i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\gamma^{\alpha}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\beta}c-i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\gamma^{\alpha}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\beta}\gamma_{5}c\,,

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}J_{1^{--}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{u^{T}_{j}Cc_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}-u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(484)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}c\,\bar{d}u-\bar{c}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}u+i\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}c-i\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c

\displaystyle-i\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}u+i\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}u-i\bar{d}\gamma_{\nu}c\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u+i\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c\,\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}u\,,

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}J_{1^{-+}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{u^{T}_{j}Cc_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}+u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(485)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}u-i\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}u-\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}u\,\bar{d}c+\bar{c}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}c

\displaystyle+i\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c\,\bar{d}\gamma_{\nu}u-i\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u-i\bar{d}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}u+i\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}u\,,

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}J_{1^{--}}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{s^{T}_{j}Cc_{k}\bar{s}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}-s^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{s}_{m}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(486)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}c\,\bar{s}s-\bar{c}c\,\bar{s}\gamma^{\mu}s+i\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}s\,\bar{s}i\gamma_{5}c-i\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}s\,\bar{s}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c

\displaystyle-i\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{s}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}s+i\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{s}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}s-i\bar{s}\gamma_{\nu}c\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}s+i\bar{s}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c\,\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}s\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{s^{T}_{j}Cc_{k}\bar{s}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}+s^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{s}_{m}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(487)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{s}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}s-i\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{s}i\gamma_{5}s-\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}s\,\bar{s}c+\bar{c}s\,\bar{s}\gamma^{\mu}c

\displaystyle+i\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}c\,\bar{s}\gamma_{\nu}s-i\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}c\,\bar{s}\sigma^{\mu\nu}s-i\bar{s}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}s+i\bar{s}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{c}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}s\,,

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}J_{2^{++}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}\left\{u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\mu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\nu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}+u^{T}_{j}C\gamma^{\nu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\right\}\,,

(488)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma_{5}u+\bar{c}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}u-\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\nu}u-\bar{c}\gamma^{\nu}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}u+\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma_{5}c

\displaystyle+\bar{c}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}c-\bar{c}\gamma^{\mu}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\nu}c-\bar{c}\gamma^{\nu}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\mu}c+g_{\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\alpha}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\nu\beta}u+g_{\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\sigma^{\nu\alpha}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\beta}u

\displaystyle+g_{\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\sigma^{\mu\alpha}u\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\nu\beta}c+g_{\alpha\beta}\bar{c}\sigma^{\nu\alpha}u\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\mu\beta}c+g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}c\,\bar{d}u+g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}u+g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}\gamma_{\alpha}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\alpha}u

\displaystyle-g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}c\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\alpha}\gamma_{5}u-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}c\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\alpha\beta}u+g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}u\,\bar{d}c+g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}c

\displaystyle+g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}\gamma_{\alpha}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\alpha}c-g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\alpha}\gamma_{5}c-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\bar{c}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}u\,\bar{d}\sigma^{\alpha\beta}c\,,

\displaystyle J_{0^{++}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{ijk}\varepsilon^{imn}u^{T}_{j}C\gamma_{\mu}c_{k}\bar{d}_{m}\gamma^{\mu}C\bar{c}^{T}_{n}\,,

\displaystyle+\bar{c}u\,\bar{d}c+\bar{c}i\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}i\gamma_{5}c+\frac{1}{2}\bar{c}\gamma_{\alpha}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\alpha}c-\frac{1}{2}\bar{c}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}u\,\bar{d}\gamma^{\alpha}\gamma_{5}c\,.
